# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

34862 [main] INFO  du.ustb.sei.mde.mwe2.EcoreGenerator  - LLM Response:
{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@697d6ded (name: calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Computes the average weekly working hours of all worker (Worker) employees in this department. The result is the arithmetic mean of weeklyWorkingHour for every Worker instance in this department's employee list. Returns 0.00 if the department has no workers. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalHours = 0 and workerCount = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     a. If employee instanceof Worker:
        - totalHours += employee.weeklyWorkingHour
        - workerCount += 1
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.00.
  4. average = totalHours / workerCount (as a double).
  5. Return Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>), Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker
Input: None (operates on the receiver Department instance).
Output: double - the average weekly working hours of all workers in the department, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.00 when the department has no workers.
Pre-condition: The Department instance must be initialized; this.employees must not be null (may be empty).
Post-condition: Returns a non-negative double. The internal state of the department and its employees remains unchanged., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@160b4809 (name: calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. The total is the sum of the holidayPremium values of every ShiftWorker in the company's employee list. Returns 0.00 if no shift workers exist. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalPremiums = 0.0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     a. If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
        - totalPremiums += ((ShiftWorker) employee).calculateHolidayPremium()
  3. Return Math.round(totalPremiums * 100.0) / 100.0

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>

    company : Company

    position : String

    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}

    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}

    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}

    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}

    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. The total is the sum of the holidayPremium values of every ShiftWorker in the company's employee list. Returns 0.00 if no shift workers exist. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalPremiums = 0.0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     a. If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
        - totalPremiums += ((ShiftWorker) employee).calculateHolidayPremium()
  3. Return Math.round(totalPremiums * 100.0) / 100.0
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>), ShiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium(), ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
Input: None
Output: double - the total holiday premium paid to all shift workers, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.00 when there are no shift workers.
Pre-condition: The Company instance must be initialized; this.employees must not be null (may be empty).
Post-condition: Returns a non-negative double representing the rounded total holiday premiums. The internal state of the company and its employees remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary: Calculates the total salary of all employees in the company. The total salary is the sum of salaries of all workers (off-shift and shift), all salespeople, and all managers. Workers' salary = weeklyWorkingHour × hourlyRates (plus holiday premiums for shift workers); Salespeople's salary = salary + amountOfSales × commissionPercentage; Managers' salary = salary. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     a. If employee instanceof Worker:
        - base = employee.weeklyWorkingHour * employee.hourlyRates
        - If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
            base += ((ShiftWorker) employee).calculateHolidayPremium()
        - totalSalary += base
     b. Else if employee instanceof SalesPeople:
        - sp = (SalesPeople) employee
        - totalSalary += sp.salary + sp.amountOfSales * sp.commissionPercentage
     c. Else if employee instanceof Manager:
        - totalSalary += ((Manager) employee).salary
  3. Return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>), Worker.weeklyWorkingHour, Worker.hourlyRates, ShiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium(), SalesPeople.salary, SalesPeople.amountOfSales, SalesPeople.commissionPercentage, Manager.salary
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
Input: None
Output: double - the total salary of all employees in the company, rounded to two decimal places.
Pre-condition: The Company instance must be initialized; this.employees must not be null (may be empty).
Post-condition: Returns a non-negative double representing the rounded total salary. The internal state of the company and its employees remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary: Calculates the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. The total commission is the sum of (amountOfSales × commissionPercentage) for every SalesPeople instance among the company's employees. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     a. If employee instanceof SalesPeople:
        - sp = (SalesPeople) employee
        - totalCommission += sp.amountOfSales * sp.commissionPercentage
  3. Return Math.round(totalCommission * 100.0) / 100.0
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>), SalesPeople.amountOfSales, SalesPeople.commissionPercentage
Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
Input: None
Output: double - the total commission earned by all salespeople, rounded to two decimal places.
Pre-condition: The Company instance must be initialized; this.employees must not be null (may be empty).
Post-condition: Returns a non-negative double representing the rounded total commission. The internal state of the company and its employees remains unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Manager::getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount
Summary: Returns the number of direct subordinate employees managed by this manager. The count reflects the size of the manager's subordinates list.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.subordinates is null, return 0.
  2. Return this.subordinates.size().
Related Features: subordinates (List<Employee>)
Related Classes: Employee
Input: None
Output: int - the number of direct subordinate employees under this manager. A value of 0 means the manager has no direct subordinates.
Pre-condition: The Manager instance must be initialized.
Post-condition: Returns a non-negative int. The internal state of the manager remains unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
SalesPeople::getTotalCommission
Summary: Computes the commission earned by this salesperson. The commission equals the amount of sales multiplied by the commission percentage.
Algorithm:
  1. Return this.amountOfSales * this.commissionPercentage.
Related Features: amountOfSales, commissionPercentage
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: double - the commission amount for this salesperson (amountOfSales × commissionPercentage). Non-negative if both inputs are non-negative.
Pre-condition: The SalesPeople instance must be initialized; amountOfSales and commissionPercentage must be set with valid numeric values.
Post-condition: Returns a double representing the commission. The internal state of the salesperson remains unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
Summary: Computes the average weekly working hours of all worker (Worker) employees in this department. The result is the arithmetic mean of weeklyWorkingHour for every Worker instance in this department's employee list. Returns 0.00 if the department has no workers. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalHours = 0 and workerCount = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
     a. If employee instanceof Worker:
        - totalHours += employee.weeklyWorkingHour
        - workerCount += 1
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.00.
  4. average = totalHours / workerCount (as a double).
  5. Return Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>), Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker
Input: None (operates on the receiver Department instance).
Output: double - the average weekly working hours of all workers in the department, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.00 when the department has no workers.
Pre-condition: The Department instance must be initialized; this.employees must not be null (may be empty).
Post-condition: Returns a non-negative double. The internal state of the department and its employees remains unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Each shift worker's premium is obtained from its calculateHolidayPremium() method. The returned value is rounded to two decimal places. If there are no shift workers in the company, 0 is returned.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
         totalPremium += ((ShiftWorker)employee).calculateHolidayPremium()
  3. Round totalPremium to 2 decimal places (e.g., using Math.round(totalPremium * 100.0) / 100.0).
  4. Return totalPremium.
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>)
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
Input: None
Output: double — the total holiday premiums of all shift workers in the company, rounded to two decimal places (>= 0.00; 0.00 if there are no shift workers).
Pre-condition: The company instance has been properly initialized and its employees list is non-null (may be empty). All ShiftWorker instances have their holidayPremium attribute set.
Post-condition: The total holiday premium is computed and returned without modifying any internal state. The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary: Calculates the total salary of all employees in the company. The total salary is the sum of salaries of all workers (off-shift and shift), all salespeople, and all managers. Off-shift workers' salary is weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates. Shift workers' salary is weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates plus holidayPremium. Salespeople's salary is fixed salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary is the fixed salary. The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize total = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       a. If employee instanceof Worker (OffShiftWorker or ShiftWorker):
            baseSalary = employee.weeklyWorkingHour * employee.hourlyRates
            If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
              total += baseSalary + ((ShiftWorker)employee).calculateHolidayPremium()
            Else:
              total += baseSalary
       b. Else if employee instanceof SalesPeople:
            total += ((SalesPeople)employee).salary
                  + ((SalesPeople)employee).amountOfSales
                  * ((SalesPeople)employee).commissionPercentage
       c. Else if employee instanceof Manager:
            total += ((Manager)employee).salary
  3. Round total to 2 decimal places (e.g., using Math.round(total * 100.0) / 100.0).
  4. Return total.
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>)
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
Input: None
Output: double — the total salary of all employees in the company, rounded to two decimal places (>= 0.00).
Pre-condition: The company instance has been properly initialized and its employees list is non-null (may be empty). All contained Employee instances have their salary-related attributes set.
Post-condition: The total salary is computed and returned without modifying any internal state. The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary: Calculates the total commission amount paid to all salespeople in the company. The commission for each salesperson is amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       If employee instanceof SalesPeople:
         totalCommission += ((SalesPeople)employee).amountOfSales
                         * ((SalesPeople)employee).commissionPercentage
  3. Round totalCommission to 2 decimal places (e.g., using Math.round(totalCommission * 100.0) / 100.0).
  4. Return totalCommission.
Related Features: employees (List<Employee>)
Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
Input: None
Output: double — the total commission amount of all salespeople in the company, rounded to two decimal places (>= 0.00; 0.00 if there are no salespeople).
Pre-condition: The company instance has been properly initialized and its employees list is non-null (may be empty). All SalesPeople instances have amountOfSales and commissionPercentage set.
Post-condition: The total commission is computed and returned without modifying any internal state. The returned value is rounded to two decimal places.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}

    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}

    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

```plantuml
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates

SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary:
  Calculates the total amount of holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company.
  Each shift worker contributes its individual holiday premium (via calculateHolidayPremium()).
  The result is rounded to two decimal places; if there are no shift workers the result is 0.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalPremiums = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       2.1. If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
             - totalPremiums += employee.calculateHolidayPremium()
  3. Round totalPremiums to two decimal places.
  4. Return totalPremiums.
Related Features:
  - Company.employees
  - ShiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium()
Related Classes: ShiftWorker, Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Company instance (its employees list).
Output:
  double - The total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers, rounded to two decimal places.
           Returns 0.0 if the company has no shift workers.
Pre-condition:
  - The Company instance must be properly initialized.
  - The employees list may be null or empty; in either case the method returns 0.0.
  - Every ShiftWorker must satisfy the department constraint (department == "Delivery") enforced
    by ShiftWorker.setdepartment(...), and must have a non-negative holidayPremium.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Company and its employees is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded sum of the holiday premiums of all shift workers.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary:
  Calculates the total salary of all employees working for the company. The total salary is
  the sum of the salaries of all workers (both off-shift and shift), all salespeople, and all
  managers. Workers' salary is computed as weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates, with shift workers
  receiving an additional holiday premium. Salespeople's salary is fixed salary plus commission
  (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage). Managers receive their fixed salary. The result is
  rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       2.1. If employee instanceof Worker (i.e., OffShiftWorker or ShiftWorker):
             - workerBase = employee.weeklyWorkingHour * employee.hourlyRates
             - If employee instanceof ShiftWorker:
                   workerBase += employee.calculateHolidayPremium()
             - totalSalary += workerBase
       2.2. Else if employee instanceof SalesPeople:
             - totalSalary += employee.salary
                               + (employee.amountOfSales * employee.commissionPercentage)
       2.3. Else if employee instanceof Manager:
             - totalSalary += employee.salary
  3. Round totalSalary to two decimal places.
  4. Return totalSalary.
Related Features:
  - Company.employees
  - Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
  - Worker.hourlyRates
  - ShiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium()
  - SalesPeople.salary
  - SalesPeople.amountOfSales
  - SalesPeople.commissionPercentage
  - Manager.salary
Related Classes: Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager, Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Company instance (its employees list).
Output:
  double - The total salary of all employees in the company, rounded to two decimal places.
           Returns 0.0 if the company has no employees.
Pre-condition:
  - The Company instance must be properly initialized.
  - The employees list may be null or empty; in either case the method returns 0.0.
  - All employees referenced must have valid numeric attributes (hourlyRates, weeklyWorkingHour,
    salary, amountOfSales, commissionPercentage, holidayPremium >= 0).
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Company and its employees is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded sum of all individual employees' salaries.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary:
  Calculates the total commission amount earned by all salespeople in the company. The commission
  of a single salesperson is amountOfSales * commissionPercentage. The result is rounded to
  two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       2.1. If employee instanceof SalesPeople:
             - totalCommission += employee.getTotalCommission()
  3. Round totalCommission to two decimal places.
  4. Return totalCommission.
Related Features:
  - Company.employees
  - SalesPeople.getTotalCommission()
Related Classes: SalesPeople, Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Company instance (its employees list).
Output:
  double - The total commission amount earned by all salespeople, rounded to two decimal places.
           Returns 0.0 if the company has no salespeople.
Pre-condition:
  - The Company instance must be properly initialized.
  - The employees list may be null or empty; in either case the method returns 0.0.
  - Every SalesPeople in the list must have non-negative amountOfSales and commissionPercentage.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Company and its employees is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded sum of the commissions of all salespeople.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

```plantuml
    weekendPermit : boolean
```

# Operation Specifications
Manager::getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount
Summary:
  Returns the number of employees that directly report to this manager (i.e., the size of
  the manager's subordinates list). Returns 0 when the manager has no direct subordinates.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.subordinates is null, return 0.
  2. Return this.subordinates.size().
Related Features:
  - Manager.subordinates
Related Classes: Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Manager instance (its subordinates list).
Output:
  int - The number of direct subordinate employees. Returns 0 when the subordinates list is
        null or empty.
Pre-condition:
  - The Manager instance must be properly initialized.
  - The subordinates list may be null or empty.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Manager and its subordinates is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the size of the subordinates list (or 0 if null/empty).

# Operation Specifications
Manager::getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount
Summary:
  Returns the number of employees that directly report to this manager (i.e., the size of
  the manager's subordinates list). Returns 0 when the manager has no direct subordinates.
Algorithm:
  1. If this.subordinates is null, return 0.
  2. Return this.subordinates.size().
Related Features:
  - Manager.subordinates
Related Classes: Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Manager instance (its subordinates list).
Output:
  int - The number of direct subordinate employees. Returns 0 when the subordinates list is
        null or empty.
Pre-condition:
  - The Manager instance must be properly initialized.
  - The subordinates list may be null or empty.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Manager and its subordinates is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the size of the subordinates list (or 0 if null/empty).

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
SalesPeople::getTotalCommission
Summary:
  Calculates and returns the commission earned by this single salesperson, defined as
  amountOfSales * commissionPercentage. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. commission = this.amountOfSales * this.commissionPercentage.
  2. Round commission to two decimal places.
  3. Return commission.
Related Features:
  - SalesPeople.amountOfSales
  - SalesPeople.commissionPercentage
Related Classes: None
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the SalesPeople instance.
Output:
  double - The commission amount of this salesperson (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage),
           rounded to two decimal places.
Pre-condition:
  - The SalesPeople instance must be properly initialized.
  - amountOfSales and commissionPercentage are expected to be non-negative numeric values.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the SalesPeople instance is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded product of amountOfSales and commissionPercentage.

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
Summary:
  Calculates the average weekly working hours of all workers belonging to this department.
  Workers include both off-shift workers and shift workers. If the department contains no
  workers, the method returns 0. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalHours = 0 and workerCount = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       2.1. If employee instanceof Worker:
             - totalHours += employee.weeklyWorkingHour
             - workerCount += 1
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.0.
  4. average = totalHours / workerCount.
  5. Round average to two decimal places.
  6. Return average.
Related Features:
  - Department.employees
  - Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker, Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Department instance (its employees list).
Output:
  double - The average weekly working hours of all workers in the department, rounded to two
           decimal places. Returns 0.0 when the department has no workers.
Pre-condition:
  - The Department instance must be properly initialized.
  - The employees list may be null or empty; in that case the method returns 0.0.
  - Every Worker in the list must have a non-negative weeklyWorkingHour.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Department and its employees is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded average weekly working hours, or 0.0 when no workers
    are present.

```result
 * revised description for operation_1
 */

class_1::operation_2
/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...
```

  Workers include both off-shift workers and shift workers. If the department contains no
  workers, the method returns 0. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalHours = 0 and workerCount = 0.
  2. For each employee in this.employees:
       2.1. If employee instanceof Worker:
             - totalHours += employee.weeklyWorkingHour
             - workerCount += 1
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.0.
  4. average = totalHours / workerCount.
  5. Round average to two decimal places.
  6. Return average.
Related Features:
  - Department.employees
  - Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Worker, OffShiftWorker, ShiftWorker, Employee
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the Department instance (its employees list).
Output:
  double - The average weekly working hours of all workers in the department, rounded to two
           decimal places. Returns 0.0 when the department has no workers.
Pre-condition:
  - The Department instance must be properly initialized.
  - The employees list may be null or empty; in that case the method returns 0.0.
  - Every Worker in the list must have a non-negative weeklyWorkingHour.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the Department and its employees is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded average weekly working hours, or 0.0 when no workers
    are present.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
ShiftWorker::calculateHolidayPremium
Summary:
  Returns the holiday premium value associated with this single shift worker. The result is
  rounded to two decimal places. This value is used both when computing a shift worker's
  individual salary and when summing up the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers.
Algorithm:
  1. premium = this.holidayPremium.
  2. Round premium to two decimal places.
  3. Return premium.
Related Features:
  - ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
Related Classes: None
Input:
  None. The operation reads the current state of the ShiftWorker instance.
Output:
  double - The holiday premium for this shift worker, rounded to two decimal places.
Pre-condition:
  - The ShiftWorker instance must be properly initialized.
  - The shift worker's department is expected to be "Delivery" (enforced by setdepartment).
  - holidayPremium is expected to be a non-negative numeric value.
Post-condition:
  - The state of the ShiftWorker instance is unchanged.
  - The returned value equals the rounded value of the stored holidayPremium.

2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary: Calculates the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. The total is the sum of holiday premiums for every ShiftWorker employee. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.
  2. Iterate through each employee in the company's employees list:
     a. If employee is an instance of ShiftWorker: totalPremium += shiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium().
  3. Round totalPremium to two decimal places using Math.round(totalPremium * 100) / 100.0.
  4. Return totalPremium.
Related Features: employees : List<Employee>
Related Classes: ShiftWorker, Employee
Input: None
Output: double - the total holiday premium paid to all shift workers in the company, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if there are no shift workers.
Pre-condition: The company's employees list must be initialized (may be empty). Each ShiftWorker must have a valid holidayPremium value.
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all shift workers' holiday premiums rounded to two decimal places. The company's state remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary: Calculates the total salary of all employees in the company. The total salary is the sum of salaries of all workers (including shift workers' holiday premiums), salespeople (including commission), and managers. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.
  2. Iterate through each employee in the company's employees list:
     a. If employee is a Manager: totalSalary += manager.salary.
     b. If employee is a SalesPeople: totalSalary += salesPeople.salary + salesPeople.amountOfSales * salesPeople.commissionPercentage.
     c. If employee is a Worker:
        i.  workerSalary = worker.weeklyWorkingHour * worker.hourlyRates.
        ii. If worker is a ShiftWorker: workerSalary += shiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium().
        iii. totalSalary += workerSalary.
  3. Round totalSalary to two decimal places using Math.round(totalSalary * 100) / 100.0.
  4. Return totalSalary.
Related Features: employees : List<Employee>
Related Classes: Employee, Manager, SalesPeople, Worker, ShiftWorker
Input: None
Output: double - the total salary of all employees in the company, rounded to two decimal places.
Pre-condition: The company's employees list must be initialized (may be empty). Each employee in the list must have its salary-related fields properly set.
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all employees' salaries rounded to two decimal places. The company's state remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary: Calculates the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. The total commission is the sum of (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage) for every SalesPeople employee. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.
  2. Iterate through each employee in the company's employees list:
     a. If employee is an instance of SalesPeople: totalCommission += salesPeople.getTotalCommission().
  3. Round totalCommission to two decimal places using Math.round(totalCommission * 100) / 100.0.
  4. Return totalCommission.
Related Features: employees : List<Employee>
Related Classes: SalesPeople, Employee
Input: None
Output: double - the total commission amount of all salespeople in the company, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if there are no salespeople.
Pre-condition: The company's employees list must be initialized (may be empty). Each SalesPeople must have valid amountOfSales and commissionPercentage values.
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all salespeople's commissions rounded to two decimal places. The company's state remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums

  2. Iterate through each employee in the company's employees list:
     a. If employee is an instance of ShiftWorker: totalPremium += shiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium().
  3. Round totalPremium to two decimal places using Math.round(totalPremium * 100) / 100.0.
  4. Return totalPremium.
Related Features: employees : List<Employee>
Related Classes: ShiftWorker, Employee
Input: None
Output: double - the total holiday premium paid to all shift workers in the company, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if there are no shift workers.
Pre-condition: The company's employees list must be initialized (may be empty). Each ShiftWorker must have a valid holidayPremium value.
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all shift workers' holiday premiums rounded to two decimal places. The company's state remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary: Calculates the total salary of all employees in the company. The total salary is the sum of salaries of all workers (including shift workers' holiday premiums), salespeople (including commission), and managers. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.
  2. Iterate through each employee in the company's employees list:
     a. If employee is a Manager: totalSalary += manager.salary.
     b. If employee is a SalesPeople: totalSalary += salesPeople.salary + salesPeople.amountOfSales * salesPeople.commissionPercentage.
     c. If employee is a Worker:
        i.  workerSalary = worker.weeklyWorkingHour * worker.hourlyRates.
        ii. If worker is a ShiftWorker: workerSalary += shiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium().
        iii. totalSalary += workerSalary.
  3. Round totalSalary to two decimal places using Math.round(totalSalary * 100) / 100.0.
  4. Return totalSalary.
Related Features: employees : List<Employee>
Related Classes: Employee, Manager, SalesPeople, Worker, ShiftWorker
Input: None
Output: double - the total salary of all employees in the company, rounded to two decimal places.
Pre-condition: The company's employees list must be initialized (may be empty). Each employee in the list must have its salary-related fields properly set.
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all employees' salaries rounded to two decimal places. The company's state remains unchanged.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary: Calculates the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. The total commission is the sum of (amountOfSales * commissionPercentage) for every SalesPeople employee. The result is rounded to two decimal places.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.
  2. Iterate through each employee in the company's employees list:
     a. If employee is an instance of SalesPeople: totalCommission += salesPeople.getTotalCommission().
  3. Round totalCommission to two decimal places using Math.round(totalCommission * 100) / 100.0.
  4. Return totalCommission.
Related Features: employees : List<Employee>
Related Classes: SalesPeople, Employee
Input: None
Output: double - the total commission amount of all salespeople in the company, rounded to two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if there are no salespeople.
Pre-condition: The company's employees list must be initialized (may be empty). Each SalesPeople must have valid amountOfSales and commissionPercentage values.
Post-condition: Returns the sum of all salespeople's commissions rounded to two decimal places. The company's state remains unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

RETURN round(holidayPremium, 2)
Related Features: 
ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: 
double - the monetary holiday premium for this shift worker, rounded to two decimal places 
(e.g., 150.00). Returns 0.0 if no holiday premium is assigned.
Pre-condition: 
The ShiftWorker instance exists with a valid holidayPremium value (>= 0). 
The shift worker must be assigned to the "Delivery" department (enforced by setDepartment).
Post-condition: 
A non-negative double value representing the holiday premium rounded to two decimal places is returned. 
No state of the shift worker is modified.}

3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;

5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;

8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.
